Revised BASE ARR is $3.8k at M18 and $7.1k at M36; it never breaks even, cash is below $0 from M12, and cash is -$95.7k at M36 (GLUT -$90.8k, SEVERE -$90.9k). The kill test's best case still ends at -$85.4k. · Doable by this founder: No. It needs a 24/7 kernel responder with a 1-2 h SLA, which a part-time founder with a day job cannot be or afford. The only ceiling-changing route is a channel of >=4,500-7,900 pure-DDR5 nodes, and an operator that size has its own kernel staff.

### RT-VC-DD4-01 — FATAL — VC — §2.4 'best case still misses the bar' / §2.5 / §10 kill experiment budget

> No outcome of the ~$6k kill experiment can turn DD4 into a venture or even a salary. Every lever +30% gives $313k ARR at M36. BASE never takes the founder full-time. The funnel yields 0.37 customers a month against the ~2.5 needed. The 2028 turn arrives at M15 with ~2.6 customers, and SEVERE runs the C-74 subscription at −3% GM. The author's own recommendation is 'demote'. Spending ~15% of the founder's capital ($6k, up to a $10k cap) and 50+ founder hours to confirm a ceiling the model already proves is poor capital allocation.

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* A pass condition that changes the ceiling, added to §10 and tested in the 29 days: for example, a channel partner (Advanced Clustering, a refurbished-server reseller, or an MSP with at least 2,000 DDR5 nodes) committing in writing to resell at volume. Without that, kill DD4 now at $0.

**Response — ACCEPTED-KILL:** Valid; I accept the kill. The fully revised model shows no PASS outcome changes the ceiling. The kill test's best case (10% incremental reclaim, 60% DDR5) gives BASE ARR $7.8k at M18 and $14.8k at M36, with cash at -$85.4k at M36. Everything +30% gives $21.1k ARR and -$23.2k. The only route that changes the ceiling is the channel the reviewer names. Model §7 puts the bar at ~4,500 (10% reclaim) to ~7,900 (6% reclaim) pure-DDR5 VM-host nodes at a 25% revenue share, just to carry one $10k/month salary plus fixed ops at peak BASE prices, and ~43% more at M36 prices. An operator that size has its own kernel staff, so the condition contradicts the target buyer. Following the Phase 4 plan, the founder would spend ~$31.3k before the first gate (M9) stopped them. Cancelling the ~$6k test (cap $10k) saves 15-25% of their capital.

*Action taken:* §10 experiment cancelled; C-74 killed at $0 on 2026-11-01 (§11 criterion 0). §10.2 adds a $0 reopen watch to 2027-03-31: a partner commits in writing to >=4,500 majority-DDR5 VM-host nodes with PO-tied savings-share invoicing, AND a <=$1k/month on-call retainer is signed. Model §7 now prints the channel threshold.

### RT-HYP-DD4-01 — MAJOR — HYP — §2.1 value driver / INPUTS savings_share 0.30, node_expansion_pm 0.005

> Savings-share pricing needs an auditable baseline. ProsperOps and Zesty bill on savings that show up on a cloud invoice. On-prem, 'DIMMs not bought' is a counterfactual: installed DRAM is a sunk cost, so value appears only at purchase events (growth or refresh). The model charges $7.36 per node per month on every installed node from day 1, yet its own node_expansion_pm implies only ~20% fleet growth over 36 months, and shortage-era buyers are stretching refresh cycles. No procurement team I know signs 30% of an unverifiable counterfactual. They push to a per-node licence and benchmark it against the RHEL or Ubuntu Pro support they already pay for, which includes `tuned`. At a savings_share of 0.20, ARR at m36 is $49.8k (my run).

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* Two pilot SOWs that accept a savings-share invoice tied to a measurable event, such as a deferred PO or a smaller DIMM SKU on new nodes.

**Response — ACCEPTED-DOWNGRADE:** Valid. ProsperOps and Zesty bill savings that appear on a cloud invoice. On-prem, installed DRAM is sunk and value appears only at growth or refresh purchases; the Phase 4 model billed every installed node from day 1. Reproduced: savings_share 0.20 on Phase 4 inputs gives ARR M36 $49.8k. I set savings_share 0.30 -> 0.20 and changed billing to invoice against a measurable event (a deferred PO or a smaller DIMM SKU on new nodes). Pilots with no such event do not convert (pilot_to_sub 0.40, shared with RT-PHY-DD4-01/RT-VET-DD4-01). With all accepted changes the fee is $1.66/node/month at peak. That is ~$20/node/year, about 4% of Ubuntu Pro's $500/server/year, which is where a procurement benchmark against existing support contracts would land anyway.

*Action taken:* Model: savings_share 0.30 -> 0.20. DD §2.1/§4: billing tied to measured PO events on 12-month contracts. The reviewer's condition (pilot SOWs accepting a savings-share invoice tied to a deferred PO or smaller SKU) is in the §10.3 PASS.

### RT-HYP-DD4-02 — MAJOR — HYP — §3 first-ten rows 1-2 / INPUTS reclaim_frac 0.10

> Proxmox VE ships with KSM on by default, and ksmtuned starts merging at about 80% host memory use (Proxmox wiki; forum, KSM_THRES_COEF=20). The lead targets, the Proxmox MSPs RichWeb and Petronella, already get the dedup slice. The extra reclaim must come from zswap and proactive reclaim on hosts running tenant VMs, where the MSP does not own guest SLOs. The 10% assumption is measured against a non-default baseline. At 6% reclaim, ARR at m36 is $46.0k (my run).

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* Kill-test host (a) showing at least 10% incremental reclaim over a stock Proxmox baseline (KSM on, ksmtuned defaults, ballooning on) at the same p99.

**Response — ACCEPTED-DOWNGRADE:** Valid; same evidence as RT-VET-DD4-02 (Proxmox forum snippets: ksmtuned on by default and activating at ~80% host RAM). The 10% was measured against a non-default baseline. Reproduced: 6% reclaim on Phase 4 inputs gives ARR M36 $46.0k. I adopted 6% as the incremental figure.

*Action taken:* Model: reclaim_frac 0.10 -> 0.06. §10.3 test hosts: (a) stock Proxmox (KSM on, ksmtuned defaults, ballooning on) as the baseline, (b) a multi-tenant host with KSM off, (c) a production MSP host. PASS requires >=10% incremental reclaim at the same p99.

### RT-HYP-DD4-03 — MAJOR — HYP — §1 product / §5 'Kubernetes segment' / KEP-2400 row

> On Kubernetes, freed physical pages create no schedulable capacity. kube-scheduler bin-packs on requests, not usage, so zswap or DAMON reclaim leaves node allocatable unchanged. Pod density rises only if requests are cut, which is right-sizing: the job of ScaleOps, Kubecost, and the free VPA and KRR tools. The K8s value story therefore depends on the product the DD has already conceded to ScaleOps.

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* A pilot design in which the customer accepts request reductions alongside the agent's reclaim and pod density rises measurably, with the buyer paying more than free VPA or KRR would cost.

**Response — ACCEPTED-DOWNGRADE:** Valid. kube-scheduler bin-packs on requests, not usage. Freed physical pages leave node allocatable unchanged, and with LimitedSwap the scheduler still packs on requests. Pod density rises only when requests drop. That is right-sizing, which belongs to ScaleOps, Kubecost and the free VPA/KRR, and Phase 4 already conceded it. Physical reclaim on K8s lowers OOM and eviction risk but creates no capacity a buyer would pay a savings share for.

*Action taken:* DD §3 buyer definition: on-prem Kubernetes removed. §5: Kubernetes segment exited now, no longer 'when ScaleOps adds swap'. §10.3: Kubernetes node removed from the test and K8s teams removed from targets. §11 criterion 10 updated. Scope is Proxmox/KVM VM hosts only.

### RT-HYP-DD4-04 — MAJOR — HYP — INPUTS oncall_small $750, insurance_pm $200 / §2.2 loaded margin / C-74 standalone

> C-74, which the DD calls 'the product', cannot fund itself. With C-75 off, as the DD recommends, and C-73 at zero, cash bottoms at −$10.8k and ends m36 at −$9.4k (my run). $750 a month buys about 4.3 contractor hours, which cannot staff 24/7 response for an agent that changes memory behavior on production nodes. Any infrastructure vendor I approve must show a named on-call rotation. With $2,000 a month of on-call from the first customer and $600 a month of E&O, the cash low is −$4.5k (my run).

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* A signed contractor 24/7 response SLA at no more than $750 a month, and a bound E&O quote at no more than $200 a month that covers production-impact claims.

**Response — ACCEPTED-KILL:** Valid, and worse than stated. Reproduced: with C-75 off and C-73 at zero, the cash low is -$10.8k at M20 and M36 cash is -$9.4k. The reviewer's -$4.5k figure (with $2,000 on-call and $600 E&O) is for the full bundle with C-73 and C-75 on. For C-74 standalone, those costs give -$40.6k at M35 (my run). Search found small-tech E&O averaging $67-110 a month (Insureon) and a startup median of ~$308 a month (Vouch via QuoteSweep), so I set E&O to $400, not $600, with the uplift for limits that cover production-impact claims. I set on-call to $2,500/$3,000. I turned C-75 off in the headline because the DD itself recommends dropping it. Revised: the bundle ends at -$95.7k at M36, and C-74 standalone (C-73 at zero) at -$112.3k.

*Action taken:* Model: oncall_small $750 -> $2,500, oncall_large $2,000 -> $3,000, insurance_pm $200 -> $400, c75_enabled 1 -> 0. Model §6 adds a 'C-74 standalone' row. C-74 killed. The reviewer's condition (signed SLA <=$750/month plus a bound E&O quote) is replaced by the §10.2 retainer condition.

### RT-HYP-DD4-05 — MAJOR — HYP — §11 kill criterion 9 / §12 cycle test / model c74 customers by M15

> The selling window is too short. Kill criterion 9 stops acquisition once DDR5 RDIMM contract prices fall 40% below peak. The model reaches only 2.6 customers by M15, when the turn starts. With the agent ready at M6 and 0.37 customers a month, that leaves about 8 months (M7-M15) and about 3 customers before the stop rule fires. DD4 is built to stop selling just as it could start to work.

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* At least 5 signed C-74 subscriptions by 2027-12-31.

**Response — ACCEPTED-DOWNGRADE:** Valid for GLUT and SEVERE, wrong for BASE. Kill criterion 9 fires at -40% from peak, not when the turn starts. BASE's turn is only -30%, so it never fires there. GLUT crosses at M22 (2028-08) and SEVERE at M20 (2028-06), which leaves 14-16 selling months after the agent is ready, not 8. But the Phase 4 model did not implement the stop at all, which was inconsistent with §11. It now does: GLUT ARR M36 is $2.5k and SEVERE $1.2k in the revised model. The revised BASE has ~1.05 C-74 subscriptions at 2027-12, far below the reviewer's bar of 5.

*Action taken:* Model: new acq_stop_price_frac 0 -> 0.60, which stops new C-74 acquisition below 60% of peak while customers < 8; model §8 prints the stop month. New §11 criterion 8: fewer than 5 signed C-74 subscriptions by 2027-12-31 -> kill C-74.

### RT-PHY-DD4-01 — MAJOR — PHY — §2.1 value per node / §1 ledger ('51 GB freed = $883 of DDR5 you did not buy') / INPUTS: reclaim_frac, savings_share

> Server DRAM is bought in channel-balanced steps, not by the GB. Full bandwidth needs every channel populated with identical DIMMs at 1DPC (12 channels on EPYC 9004/9005, 8 on Xeon 4th/5th gen), and 2DPC derates DDR5 speed. Per-socket capacity therefore moves in 1.33-1.5× steps (e.g., 12×{32,48,64,96} GB = 384/576/768/1152 GB). A 10% reclaim lets a homogeneous fleet drop a step only if its requirement sits within ~11% above that step: P ≈ 0.11 × S_k/(S_k+1 − S_k) ≈ 22-33%. Otherwise the deferral is $0, and installed nodes never 'un-buy' DIMMs. The per-node fee bills value that most 80-node customers will never realise, which is a dispute and churn driver the model does not carry.

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* Purchase quotes from 2-3 pilot customers where measured reclaim changed the DIMM SKU or count, or a re-run billing only at procurement events with step-crossing probability applied.

**Response — ACCEPTED-DOWNGRADE:** Valid on billing and churn; partly refuted on the mean. Using the reviewer's own formula, P(step) ~ r x S_k/dS, and the saving when it happens is dS, so E[saving] = r x S_k. Across a portfolio of buyers the linear value is right on average. The problem is the distribution. At the revised 6% reclaim and 1.33-1.5x steps, P ~ 12-18%, so most DIMM-configuration buyers realize $0 on their next PO and dispute a per-node bill. Installed nodes never un-buy. Memory-bound VM hosts that buy fewer whole nodes (1/N granularity) realize value more smoothly. Modeled as lost conversion (pilot_to_sub 0.60 -> 0.40) plus billing only at procurement events. On Phase 4 inputs the conversion cut alone gives ARR M36 $49.8k.

*Action taken:* Model: pilot_to_sub 0.60 -> 0.40. DD §2.1 explains why step quantization averages out but splits buyers into winners and losers. §10.3 PASS requires the reviewer's evidence: pilot purchase quotes showing a changed DIMM SKU/count or node count.

### RT-PHY-DD4-02 — MAJOR — PHY — §10 PASS condition ('≥10% of installed RAM freed') / §11 kill 5

> The core technical threshold is undefined in a way that can pass falsely. On Linux, idle RAM fills with page cache. 'Freeing' file cache, trivially on a CI runner (workload c), raises MemFree without reducing the capacity the node needs, and it costs I/O. TMO's 20-32% is a reduction in resident memory at PSI-bounded performance, much of it anonymous memory offloaded to zswap or SSD. On three replayed workloads chosen by the vendor, '10% freed' says nothing about DIMMs not bought.

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* Define reclaim as the lowest cgroup memory.max (or VM balloon target) that holds p99 and PSI 'some' within bounds, reported as an anonymous vs file split, with the PASS bar applied to anonymous plus required-cache reduction.

**Response — ACCEPTED-MITIGATED:** Valid. MemFree rising after page cache is dropped (trivial on a CI runner) is not capacity saved, and it costs I/O. TMO's numbers are resident-memory reductions at PSI-bounded performance. I adopt the reviewer's definition. Reclaim is now the lowest cgroup memory.max (or VM balloon target) that holds p99 within 5% and PSI memory 'some' avg60 under a fixed bound for 14 days, compared with the same measure on the stock baseline and reported as an anonymous/file split. The 10% bar applies only to anonymous memory plus the cache the workload needs. The model's revised 6% already represents anonymous, compressible reclaim, so this change fixes the test without lowering prospects further.

*Action taken:* §10.3 metric redefined as above; workload (c), the CI runner, replaced by a second, multi-tenant Proxmox host with KSM off; the Kubernetes node removed. §11 criterion 6 uses the §10.3 metric. No model change.

### RT-PHY-DD4-03 — MAJOR — PHY — §3 customers #4-7 (MSU ICER, UArizona, Rutgers/UMBC/UIC, UCR) / §10 target list (15 research-computing centers)

> HPC compute nodes normally run with no swap. MPI bulk-synchronous jobs touch their whole working set every iteration, so cold anonymous memory is small, and one rank's swap-in stalls every rank. Slurm already caps each job at its requested memory through cgroups. The real saving lever is right-sizing job memory requests (seff/XDMoD), not kernel reclaim, and node purchases are set by the largest-memory job class. Four of the ten named first customers, and 15 of the 60 kill-test targets, are physically mismatched to the product.

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* One research-computing center that runs swap or zswap on compute nodes and measures ≥10% cold anonymous memory on production jobs without wall-time regression.

**Response — ACCEPTED-DOWNGRADE:** Valid; same as RT-VET-DD4-03. Swapless compute nodes, whole-working-set MPI iterations, Slurm cgroup caps at the requested size, and purchases sized for the largest-memory job class: four of the first ten customers and 15 of the 60 targets were physically mismatched. I do not pursue the reviewer's condition (a center running zswap on compute nodes with >=10% cold anonymous memory). Even then, the scheduler would not resell freed memory without oversubscription.

*Action taken:* DD §3 rows 4-7 dropped; §10.3 targets have no research-computing centers. Model: scan_to_pilot 0.40 -> 0.30 (shared with RT-VC-DD4-05, RT-VET-DD4-03).

### RT-VC-DD4-02 — MAJOR — VC — §2.1 'How the value is computed' / INPUTS contract_months=36, savings_share=0.30

> The model calls the value 'deferred' but prices it as 'avoided': 51.2 GB is valued at the full $25.78/GB, as if the DIMMs are never bought. If the operator merely defers a purchase, the saving is the price difference between buying now and buying later, plus time value. That is near zero in BASE, where prices hold through 2027, and large only if a glut arrives, when the fee basis collapses anyway. The customer is also asked to pay a 36-month recurring fee for a one-time deferral, and has every reason to cancel once the purchase decision has passed.

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* Pilot customers signing 12-month contracts where the ledger ties the saving to a specific cancelled or reduced DIMM or server PO. And a re-run with value = price(now) − PV(price at the deferred date).

**Response — ACCEPTED-DOWNGRADE:** Valid in mechanism, wrong in magnitude. A recurring fee for a one-time deferral over-charges, and the Phase 4 model also re-billed the full value at every renewal. But the reviewer's own formula is not 'near zero in BASE'. BASE has a -30% turn over 2028, so price(now) - PV(price at M36) = 25.78 - 18.05/1.26 ~ $11.4/GB, which is 44% of the avoided value: deferring into a falling market is worth more, not less. The formula also ignores that DIMMs bought later have more life left. The right measure is the rental equivalent of 36 months of capacity: P0 - PV(residual value of 3-year-old DIMMs), which is ~68% (flat prices) to 78% (BASE) [ESTIMATE: 8%/yr, 40% residual]. I set value_realization 1.0 -> 0.75; on Phase 4 inputs that alone gives ARR M36 $54.5k. Cancellation once the purchase decision has passed is covered by the lower pilot-to-subscription rate.

*Action taken:* Model: new input value_realization 1.0 -> 0.75, applied to the fee basis. DD §2.1/§4: 12-month contracts with a ledger tied to a named cancelled or reduced DIMM/server PO. §10.3 PASS requires those PO deltas.

### RT-VC-DD4-03 — MAJOR — VC — §3 customers #1–2 (Proxmox MSPs) / §1 KSM lever / INPUTS reclaim_frac=0.10

> The first contacts are Proxmox MSPs (RichWeb, Petronella), and Proxmox VE already ships KSM enabled by default through ksmtuned, which kicks in at ~80% host RAM. The Proxmox wiki also advises disabling KSM on multi-tenant hosting for security reasons (pve.proxmox.com wiki, snippet, 2026-11-01). On the beachhead, the dedup lever is either already harvested or a cross-tenant side-channel liability. That leaves zswap and proactive reclaim, where Google's fleet figure is ~5%, and at 5% reclaim M36 ARR is $40.3k (§2.4).

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* Measured incremental reclaim of at least 10% on at least 2 production Proxmox MSP hosts, with KSM left in its default state and no cross-tenant KSM enabled.

**Response — ACCEPTED-DOWNGRADE:** Valid; same finding as RT-HYP-DD4-02 and RT-VET-DD4-02. Proxmox's advice to disable KSM on multi-tenant hosting makes it worse: on hosting MSPs the KSM slice is either already harvested or should be off. Reproduced: 5% reclaim on Phase 4 inputs gives ARR M36 $40.3k. I set 6%, between Google's ~5% zswap fleet figure and HYP's 6% run; on Phase 4 inputs that gives $46.0k. With every other change applied, the fee is $1.66/node/month.

*Action taken:* Model: reclaim_frac 0.10 -> 0.06. §10.3: >=10% incremental reclaim on production Proxmox MSP hosts, with KSM in its default state and no cross-tenant KSM enabled (the reviewer's condition, extended to >=5 hosts).

### RT-VC-DD4-04 — MAJOR — VC — INPUTS oncall_small=$750/month, oncall_large=$2,000 / §2.2 loaded margin

> $750 a month for a kernel-savvy engineer on call for production memory incidents buys about 4 hours at $175/h: a best-effort email, not on-call. The VC scorer's 'one production incident from zero' needs a responder within an hour, around the clock. A credible retainer costs several thousand dollars a month, or a cofounder. That pushes loaded C-74 gross margin from 37% to negative until the 6+ customers the model reaches only after M24.

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* A signed retainer quote at $1,000/month or less with a response SLA of 1 hour or less, from a named kernel contractor.

**Response — ACCEPTED-KILL:** Valid; same as RT-VET-DD4-04 and RT-HYP-DD4-04. A 1-hour, 24/7 response needs at least two people in rotation; $750 buys a best-effort email. Revised costs: $2,500/$3,000 a month on-call plus $400 E&O. C-74 loaded GM moves from 37% to -473% on the revised model. Even with every other input at its Phase 4 value, on-call of $2,500 at 1-5 customers alone pushes the cash low to -$5.0k, breaching the $40k.

*Action taken:* Model: oncall_small $750 -> $2,500, oncall_large $2,000 -> $3,000. C-74 killed. The reviewer's condition (a signed retainer of <=$1,000/month with a <=1-2 h SLA from a named contractor) is a §10.2 reopen condition.

### RT-VC-DD4-05 — MAJOR — VC — §5 'The real C-74 competitor' / INPUTS reply_rate, conv_to_scan, scan_to_pilot, pilot_to_sub

> The real competitor is the operator's own Linux admin using free upstream features (zswap, MGLRU, PSI reclaim, Proxmox defaults, tuned). The target buyer has 'no kernel team' yet runs 20–200 nodes, and such shops are the least willing to let an outside agent change production memory behavior before seeing SOC 2, which the model books only at M20 ($12k). The precedents are bad: Intel shut Granulate in Oct-2024 and MemVerge pivoted (§5). None of the 8% × 35% × 40% × 60% funnel steps has a benchmark.

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* At least 2 signed paid-pilot LOIs at $5/node/month or more from majority-DDR5 fleets (already a PASS condition), where neither signer had zswap or tuned reclaim enabled before the scan.

**Response — ACCEPTED-DOWNGRADE:** Valid. The real competitor is the in-house admin using free zswap, MGLRU and PSI reclaim plus Proxmox defaults. A 20-200-node shop with no kernel team is the least willing to let an outside agent change production memory without SOC 2. None of the four funnel rates has an on-prem benchmark; Phase 4 already tagged them ESTIMATE. Granulate (shut by Intel, Oct-2024) and MemVerge (pivoted 2025) are the precedent. I set scan_to_pilot 0.40 -> 0.30 and the SOC 2 trigger 5 -> 2 customers, so the $12k now lands at M18. On Phase 4 inputs, the conversion cut alone gives ARR M36 $54.5k, and the earlier SOC 2 alone gives a cash low of $4.3k.

*Action taken:* Model: scan_to_pilot 0.40 -> 0.30; soc2_trigger_customers 5 -> 2. §10.3 LOI condition now requires that neither signer had zswap or tuned reclaim enabled before the scan, at >=$5/node/month from majority-DDR5 fleets.

### RT-VET-DD4-01 — MAJOR — VET — §2.1 value ledger ('51.2 GB freed = $883 of DDR5 not bought') / INPUTS node_gb, reclaim_frac

> DRAM is not bought by the GB. It is bought in DIMM quanta under channel-population rules. A 2-socket SPR/EMR or Genoa node must populate every channel (8 or 12 per socket) with identical DIMMs for full bandwidth. The step below 512 GB (16x32 GB) is 384 GB (16x24 GB non-binary), a 25% step, or 256 GB. A 10% reclaim changes no purchase order unless the fleet is memory-bound and the operator buys fewer nodes. The model prices a fractional DIMM linearly, when the real value is zero below the next DIMM step and lumpy above it.

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* From the 2 or more pilot LOIs, a written 2027 server quote or PO showing a smaller DIMM configuration or fewer nodes because of measured reclaim.

**Response — ACCEPTED-DOWNGRADE:** Valid on mechanism, partly wrong on the mean. Channel population is real: 2-socket SPR/EMR fill 16 channels and Genoa 24 at 1DPC, so a 512 GB node steps down to 384 GB (16x24 GB), a 25% step. But if a buyer's requirement lies anywhere between steps, P(dropping a step) ~ r x S_k/dS and the saving when it drops is dS, so E[saving] = r x S_k. Across many buyers the linear price is right on average; for any single buyer it is wrong. At the revised 6% reclaim, P ~ 0.06 x 384/128 = 18%: about 82% of DIMM-configuration buyers save $0 on their next PO and 18% save a full 128 GB step. Installed nodes never un-buy DIMMs. For memory-bound Proxmox hosts the lever is fewer whole nodes, at 1/N granularity. That also saves CPU, chassis and licences, an upside I did not model. So I model the step as lost conversion, not lost value: only pilots that show a PO change convert, and pilot_to_sub goes 0.60 -> 0.40. On Phase 4 inputs that change alone cuts BASE ARR M36 from $68.7k to $49.8k (model §0).

*Action taken:* Model: pilot_to_sub 0.60 -> 0.40. DD §2.1 documents step quantization as mean-neutral but bimodal. Reopen PASS (§10.3) now requires written 2027 quotes or POs showing a smaller DIMM configuration or fewer nodes because of measured reclaim.

### RT-VET-DD4-02 — MAJOR — VET — §1 ('Small operators rarely turn these on') / §3 customers #1-#2 (Proxmox MSPs)

> Proxmox VE enables KSM by default: ksmtuned starts merging at about 80% host RAM use (Proxmox wiki; forum on KSM_THRES_COEF=20). The dedup slice of the 10% is already being harvested free on the beachhead platform. The same wiki recommends disabling KSM for hosting services for security reasons. Cross-VM page dedup opens dedup side channels and Flip Feng Shui-style Rowhammer attacks (Razavi et al., USENIX Security 2016 [training]), a real multi-tenant risk on DDR4 without robust TRR. The MSP buyer is being sold a feature that is either already on or that their security posture says to turn off.

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* Scan data from 5 or more MSP Proxmox hosts showing 10% or more further reclaim from zswap and proactive reclaim alone, with KSM counted as already on (or off for security), at 5% or less p99 regression.

**Response — ACCEPTED-DOWNGRADE:** Valid. Proxmox forum sources (search, 2026-11-01, snippets only) say ksmtuned is enabled by default on ISO installs and starts merging at ~80% host RAM (KSM_THRES_COEF=20); I could not open the official wiki. On the beachhead the dedup slice is therefore either already harvested or, on multi-tenant hosts, should be off because of dedup side channels and Flip Feng Shui-style attacks. What remains is zswap plus proactive reclaim of guest memory, where Google's fleet figure is ~5%. I cut reclaim_frac from 0.10 to 0.06, counted as incremental over a stock baseline. On Phase 4 inputs that alone takes BASE ARR M36 from $68.7k to $46.0k and C-74 subscription GM from 37% to 2%.

*Action taken:* Model: reclaim_frac 0.10 -> 0.06. Policy: never enable cross-tenant KSM on multi-tenant hosts. §10.3 uses the reviewer's bar: >=10% further reclaim from zswap and proactive reclaim on >=5 MSP Proxmox hosts at <=5% p99 regression, with KSM counted as already on (or off for security).

### RT-VET-DD4-03 — MAJOR — VET — §3 rows 4-7 (research computing; contacted first per 'Order of contact')

> HPC is a poor fit for reclaim. Slurm allocates memory per job by request. Compute nodes usually run without swap. MPI codes are sensitive to OS jitter, so compression stalls show up in collectives. Node memory is specified as GB/core in a grant-funded buy to meet user requirements. Reclaiming cold pages on a running job frees nothing the scheduler can resell unless memory is oversubscribed, which HPC centers avoid, so 'more PI jobs per purchased node' is not how HPC buys.

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* One research-computing center agrees to run the agent on production compute nodes (not login nodes) with memory oversubscription enabled. Otherwise drop rows 4-7 and re-run the funnel.

**Response — ACCEPTED-DOWNGRADE:** Valid. Slurm enforces per-job memory through cgroups at the requested size. Compute nodes typically run without swap. Bulk-synchronous MPI codes touch their working set every iteration and are sensitive to jitter, so cold anonymous memory is small and compression stalls appear in collectives. Pages reclaimed from a running job create no capacity the scheduler can resell unless the center oversubscribes memory, which HPC centers avoid. The ICER and UArizona price pain is real, but the lever for it is right-sizing job memory requests, which is free. Removing the segment narrows the scope to VM hosts. Its conversion effect is folded into scan_to_pilot 0.40 -> 0.30 (shared with RT-VC-DD4-05); on Phase 4 inputs that cut alone gives BASE ARR M36 $54.5k.

*Action taken:* DD §3: rows 4-7 dropped and row 8 (Advanced Clustering, an HPC channel) demoted. §10.3 target list: the 15 research-computing centers and 5 HPC integrators replaced by Proxmox/KVM MSPs and regional colo/hosting firms. Model: scan_to_pilot 0.40 -> 0.30.

### RT-VET-DD4-04 — MAJOR — VET — INPUTS oncall_small $750/month, oncall_large $2,000 / kill criterion 4

> No one can buy a credible on-call for an agent that changes memory behavior on customers' production nodes for $750 a month. That is about 4 hours of a kernel memory-management engineer's time, with no response SLA. A real retainer with a 1-2-hour response SLA is about $3k/month [ESTIMATE: ~15 reserved h plus an availability premium]. My run with on-call at $3,000 in both tiers drives the cash low to −$11,544 (it breaches the $40k) and C-74 subscription GM to −18%. With a part-time founder at a day job, kill criterion 4 ('second incident -> kill') is one bad night from triggering.

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* A signed retainer from a named kernel engineer at $1,000/month or less with a 2-hour response SLA or better.

**Response — ACCEPTED-KILL:** Valid, and fatal to C-74 as a business line. $750 a month buys ~4.3 contractor hours with no SLA. I reproduced the reviewer's run: on-call $3,000 in both tiers on Phase 4 inputs gives cash low -$11,544 at M20 and C-74 subscription GM -18%. I set on-call to $2,500 a month at 1-5 customers and $3,000 at 6+ (midpoint of HYP's $2,000 and the reviewer's ~$3,000). On the fully revised model an 80-node customer pays $133 a month against a $728 on-call allocation, a loaded GM of -473%. On-call plus E&O alone needs ~29-34 customers at ~$100 gross profit each; the model reaches 5.4 by M36. A part-time founder with a day job cannot be the night responder. Only a kernel cofounder on equity would remove this cost, and none is in hand.

*Action taken:* Model: oncall_small $750 -> $2,500, oncall_large $2,000 -> $3,000. C-74 killed. A signed retainer of <=$1,000 a month with a <=2-hour 24/7 SLA, or an equity kernel cofounder, is now one of the two §10.2 reopen conditions.

### RT-VET-DD4-05 — MAJOR — VET — INPUTS ddr5_share 0.60 / §10 PASS ('majority DDR5')

> SMB MSP and university fleets on 4-6-year refresh cycles were still mostly DDR4 in late 2026, because DDR5 server platforms have shipped only since late 2022/2023. On installed DDR5 nodes, reclaim defers only incremental purchases, which a shortage is already delaying. Nothing sources 60% DDR5 node-GB. At ddr5_share=0.30 the model's supportable fee falls from $7.36 to $4.63/node, below the DD's own $5 pass bar. m36 ARR falls to $47,664 and C-74 subscription GM to 6%. Combined with $3k on-call, cash ends m36 at −$18,734.

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* A kill-test census of 10 or more responding fleets showing 50% or more of node-GB on DDR5, with 2027 server purchases planned.

**Response — ACCEPTED-DOWNGRADE:** Valid. Reproduced: ddr5_share 0.30 gives a fee of $4.63/node, ARR M36 $47,664 and C-74 GM 6%; adding $3k on-call gives cash M36 -$18,734. The 60% was a targeting rule, not a measurement. Search (snippets): DDR5 was ~53% of server DRAM revenue in 2025 (Mordor Intelligence), a figure inflated by DDR5's higher $/GB, and DDR4 still carried the installed base in legacy enterprise and public sector in 2025-26 (Research and Markets). VMware-to-Proxmox migrations also usually reuse existing hosts. I set ddr5_share 0.60 -> 0.40, between the reviewers' 0.30 and Phase 4. On Phase 4 inputs that alone gives ARR M36 $54.7k.

*Action taken:* Model: ddr5_share 0.60 -> 0.40. §10.3 PASS needs a census of >=10 responding fleets with >=50% of node-GB on DDR5 and 2027 server purchases planned.

### RT-HYP-DD4-06 — MINOR — HYP — INPUTS ddr5_share 0.60 / §3 first-ten list

> The first-ten list (universities, Proxmox MSPs, refurb resellers) runs on mostly DDR4 installed bases in 2026. The DD's own sources say shortage buyers are keeping DDR4 fleets and buying refurbished DDR4. At ddr5_share 0.30, ARR at m36 is $47.7k and m36 cash is $27.8k (my run). The kill test's 'majority DDR5' filter is right, but it will cut the list hard.

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* At least 15 of the 60 kill-test targets confirming majority-DDR5 fleets with 2027 purchase plans.

**Response — ACCEPTED-DOWNGRADE:** Valid; same as RT-VET-DD4-05. Reproduced: ddr5_share 0.30 gives ARR M36 $47.7k and cash M36 $27.8k. 'Majority DDR5' is the right filter, but it will shrink the list. I set 0.40.

*Action taken:* Model: ddr5_share 0.60 -> 0.40. §10.3 PASS adds the reviewer's census condition: >=15 of the 60 targets confirm majority-DDR5 fleets with 2027 purchase plans.

### RT-PHY-DD4-04 — MINOR — PHY — §1 mechanisms (zswap 2:1, KSM) / INPUTS: reclaim_frac

> Common configurations defeat the mechanisms. KSM merges only 4 KiB pages, so VMs backed by hugetlbfs or 1 GiB hugepages (a standard Proxmox and DPDK tuning) cannot be merged, and hugetlbfs pages cannot be swapped. Confidential VMs (AMD SEV-SNP, Intel TDX) present ciphertext to the host, so host-side zswap compresses at about 1:1 and KSM finds nothing. KSM is also often disabled for cross-VM side-channel reasons. A single fleet-wide 10% reclaim_frac hides a per-configuration distribution that can be zero.

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* Scan results from the first 5 installs reporting hugepage, SEV/TDX and KSM status, with reclaim modelled per configuration.

**Response — ACCEPTED-MITIGATED:** Valid. KSM merges only 4 KiB anonymous pages. VMs backed by hugetlbfs or 1 GiB pages cannot be merged or swapped. SEV-SNP/TDX guests present ciphertext to the host, so host-side zswap compresses at ~1:1 and KSM finds nothing. KSM is also often off for side-channel reasons. A single fleet-wide average hides hosts where reclaim is zero.

*Action taken:* The scanner now reports, per host, hugepage/hugetlbfs backing, SEV-SNP/TDX status and KSM state. Such hosts are excluded from billable nodes and from the reclaim average (DD §1, §10.3). The revised 6% fleet figure [ESTIMATE] assumes these are a minority of SMB MSP hosts; the first 5 installs report them, as the reviewer asks. No further model change.

### RT-PHY-DD4-05 — MINOR — PHY — §7 swap safety / §2.2 COGS (no device-wear term)

> Proactive reclaim with SSD writeback turns cold-page churn into flash writes. With 51 GB per node reclaimed and the cold set turning over 2-4 times a day, that is 100-200 GB/day. On a 480 GB boot M.2 rated around 1 DWPD [ESTIMATE: typical boot-class SKU; verify per platform], swap alone consumes 0.2-0.4 DWPD. Meta's TMO work treats device endurance as a constraint on offload. The model has no swap-device spec, no wear COGS, and no rule against swapping to boot media.

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* Pilot telemetry of swap write volume per node per day against the installed drive's rated TBW, plus a zswap-only (no writeback) policy where endurance is short.

**Response — ACCEPTED-MITIGATED:** Valid. Writing back 100-200 GB/day to a ~1 DWPD 480 GB boot M.2 consumes 0.2-0.4 DWPD. The fix is a policy, not a COGS line. On boot-class media the agent runs zswap with writeback disabled: the cgroup v2 memory.zswap.writeback=0 knob comes from the 2023-24 upstream series (LKML RFC 2023-11), and older kernels do not apply it hierarchically (LKML 2024-08), so the agent sets it per leaf cgroup. Writeback goes only to a dedicated swap device with a TBW budget. Swap writes per day are logged against rated TBW, and a host falls back automatically to zswap-only above 0.1 DWPD. Disabling writeback lowers achievable reclaim, which the 6% already reflects. With no writeback to boot media there is no wear COGS.

*Action taken:* DD §1 and §10.3 policy: zswap-only on boot media, a dedicated swap device with a TBW budget otherwise, and write telemetry. New §11 criterion 5 (drive-wear rule). No model change.

### RT-PHY-DD4-06 — MINOR — PHY — §11 kill 4 ('a second incident → kill C-74') / §2.2 loaded margin

> zswap moves anonymous user pages into kernel-owned zsmalloc memory. A DRAM uncorrectable error in a user page is usually survivable through machine-check recovery (the process is killed). The same UE in kernel pool memory typically panics the node, unless the running kernel's MCE recovery covers zswap paths. Schroeder et al. (2009) report UEs on ~1.3% of machines per year. With ~5% of RAM in the pool (10% reclaim at 2:1), a 2,157-node book (the 27-customer salary case, §2.5) sees on the order of 1-2 agent-attributable panics a year, enough to trip the two-incident kill.

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* Confirmation that the target kernels recover from poisoned zswap/zsmalloc pages without panicking, or an incident definition that excludes hardware UEs, written into the SOW.

**Response — ACCEPTED-MITIGATED:** Valid. The kernel hwpoison documentation says only LRU pages are recoverable and most kernel objects are not; zswap's zsmalloc pool is kernel memory. The 2025 'zswap: do not crash the kernel on decompression failure' patch covers detected corruption, not machine-check consumption in kernel context (merge status unconfirmed). Reproduced: 2,157 nodes x 1.3%/yr x 5% pool share ~ 1.4 panics a year. At the revised book (~456 nodes at M36, ~3% of RAM in the pool at 6% reclaim and 2:1) it is ~0.18 a year. Still, one such panic on a pilot host could have tripped the old two-incident kill.

*Action taken:* §11 criterion 4 and every SOW exclude node panics whose root cause is a hardware uncorrectable error logged by MCE/EDAC/rasdaemon, and require kdump on agent hosts so the cause can be proven (DD §7, §11).

### RT-PHY-DD4-07 — MINOR — PHY — §1 C-75 / §12 C-75 reuse-interval argument

> A host-DRAM KV tier is bounded by bandwidth, not only capacity. Llama-3-70B KV is 80 layers × 8 KV heads × 128 × 2 × 2 B ≈ 0.33 MB/token, so a 32k-token prefix is ≈10.7 GB. Eight GPUs on PCIe Gen5 x16 can pull ~440 GB/s together (~55 GB/s each), while one socket of 12-channel DDR5-4800 peaks at 460.8 GB/s theoretical (practically ~300-370 GB/s). Concurrent fetches saturate host DRAM and cross NUMA. Sizing by capacity and break-even reuse interval alone misprices the tier.

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* A sizing method that includes per-socket bandwidth and NUMA placement, validated on one 8-GPU node under concurrent prefix fetches.

**Response — ACCEPTED-MITIGATED:** Valid. The arithmetic checks. Llama-3-70B KV = 80 layers x 8 KV heads x 128 x 2 x 2 B = 0.33 MB/token, or 10.7 GB per 32k-token prefix. Eight GPUs at ~55 GB/s each over PCIe Gen5 x16 pull ~440 GB/s, while a 12-channel DDR5-4800 socket peaks at 460.8 GB/s theoretical (~300-370 GB/s practical). Under concurrent prefix fetches the host-DRAM tier is bandwidth- and NUMA-bound, and sizing by capacity and reuse interval alone misprices it. C-75 was already being dropped apart from opportunistic consulting; this adds a technical reason.

*Action taken:* Model: c75_enabled 1 -> 0 in the revised headline (C-75 on is only a sensitivity row, ARR M36 $17.7k). DD §12: any C-75 consulting sizing must include per-socket bandwidth and NUMA placement, checked on the customer's 8-GPU node under concurrent fetches.

### RT-VC-DD4-06 — MINOR — VC — §0 C-73 'cash bridge' / INPUTS c73_rate_pt=0.33

> C-73 averages about $1k a month (0.33 audits a month at $3k) from a manufacturing network that is not the SaaS buyer (the model's own note). AWS on-demand prices were unchanged across 92 SKUs, so there is no tailwind. At that rate it does not bridge anything and only takes founder hours from C-74.

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* 3 committed $3k audits from the founder's network inside the kill window; otherwise drop C-73.

**Response — ACCEPTED-DOWNGRADE:** Valid. On Phase 4 inputs C-73 averaged ~$1k a month. The founder's network is in manufacturing, not cloud SaaS, and all 92 compared EC2 on-demand SKUs were unchanged from Sep-2025 to Oct-2026, so there is no tailwind. I cut c73_rate_pt 0.33 -> 0.17, about 2 audits a year. On Phase 4 inputs that alone moves the cash low from $10.4k to $8.1k and cash at M36 from $55.7k to $39.6k. C-73 bridges nothing.

*Action taken:* Model: c73_rate_pt 0.33 -> 0.17. DD §0: C-73 is no longer called a cash bridge, only opportunistic consulting. New §11 criterion 3: fewer than 3 committed $3k audits from the founder's network by 2026-11-30 -> drop C-73.

## DD5 — U.S. Open Memory IP (compiler service -> gain-cell & ReRAM macros) (open-memory-ip)

**Final status after red team: DOWNGRADED.** The red team killed both T2 legs. C-32 (gain cell) and C-22 (ReRAM) have no named buyer and no density or leakage case on sky130, and C-22's magnet-immunity axis is shared by OTP, eFuse and S130's own embedded NVM. The C-33 wedge survives on revised inputs: 228 projects/yr and a 55-account cap; the licence restated to nominal-lot data at $12k; free GF180 macros in the base case; a $150/h exclusive contractor; and cash-gated spends. On those inputs BASE ARR is $41.2k at m18 and $65.7k at m36, and the founder is never paid in BASE, GLUT or SEVERE. It is DOWNGRADED rather than KILLED because it needs no outside capital, is cash-positive from m12, and a ~$9.1k day-30 test decides it cheaply.

*Revised verdicts* — Possible: Yes at simulation level for the C-33 wedge: a 1 KB sky130 macro is silicon-verified (TRL 6), while 16 KB+ macros are unproven and now gated on contractor-signed extracted-SPICE corners plus a 1,000-point Monte Carlo. An MPW can deliver 'nominal lot measured, corners simulated', not 'measured PVT corners'. No for T2 on this PDK: a logic-rule gain cell does not beat the pushed-rule 6T, and ReRAM's axis is shared by OTP and S130 eNVM. · Profitable: No as a job; marginal as a side line. Gross margin is 57%, and the business is cash-positive from m12 with the founder unpaid. ARR is $41.2k at m18 and $65.7k at m36 ($164.1k run-rate), and the founder is never paid in BASE, GLUT or SEVERE: peak GP is $8.0k/mo against the $11.85k needed. Only a combined upside (price x1.3, close x1.3, no free release) pays the founder, at m22. A full free release cuts ARR at m36 to $34.0k. · Doable by this founder: Yes for the wedge part-time (automation, CI, portal and sales are the founder's skills), but only with a contract memory engineer under a signed 12-month exclusivity, or a technical cofounder with equity, by m3, because the founder cannot judge the sign-off they sell. No for T2 (killed).

### RT-VC-DD5-01 — FATAL — VC — §2.2 market funnel / §2.6 'Free-bundle shock' / §2.7 #4 and #6

> The serviceable ceiling at 100% share is $747k a year, and it rests mostly on estimates. Of the 298 projects a year, 150 are the author's estimates (ChipFoundry 60, Cadence 30, MOSIS/Commons/other 60), and 54 more depend on an estimated second wafer.space run a year. Paying the founder needs ~18 accounts by m18, 25% of every account that could exist. One free release of characterized ≥16 KB macros cuts m36 ARR to $50k and the founder is never paid. OCD and UCSC committed code in the last week, and Chips4Makers taped out SRAM test chips in Jul-2026. A business whose ceiling is under $1M even with no competition, and which one free release kills, is not one I would fund.

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* Kill-test results (i) and (iv) both pass: at least 3 paid deposits, and a written statement from UCSC VLSIDA, OCD and ChipFoundry that none plans free characterized ≥16 KB macros within 18 months. Or an independent count showing at least 150 paying-capable shuttle projects a year.

**Response — ACCEPTED-DOWNGRADE:** Accepted on the numbers, not on KILL. Half of the 298 projects were my estimates, and the Phase 4 ChipFoundry input note itself said ~20-30 paid slots a year yet used 60. Revised inputs:
- ChipFoundry 30, Cadence 20, MOSIS/Commons/other 30;
- 228 projects a year, 27 paying projects a year, a 55-account cap, and a $424k/yr serviceable ceiling (was $747k);
- free is now in the base case (RT-HYP-DD5-04).

Revised BASE has ARR $41.2k at m18 and $65.7k at m36, and the founder is never paid. A full free shock gives $34.0k at m36. I do not accept KILL, because the wedge asks no one for money. It runs on $40k with a gated cash low of $4.0k, is cash-positive from m12, and a ~$9k day-30 test can kill it cheaply. I withdraw 'credible FALLBACK' and agree it is not fundable: it is a side line only.

*Action taken:* Model R1-R3 (market 298 -> 228 projects/yr) and R13 (base-case free release). Section 0 verdict changed from 'FALLBACK or side line' to 'side line only'. Section 2.2 ceiling $747k -> $424k. Section 7: never raise. Section 10 (iv) requires written statements.

### RT-VC-DD5-02 — FATAL — VC — §2.8 T2 overlay / §5 RAAAM and Toggle rows / 03_SCORING §7.7 (T2 slot)

> The T2 stage (C-32/C-22) is the only thing that makes DD5 more than C-33, and it fails on its own numbers. In silicon, gain cells give 1.25–1.43× bitcell density, a small step next to the 5.5× macro-density gain the wedge already sells by banking SRAM, so a customer short of area gets more from the wedge than from the product. RAAAM holds the commercial seat and the patent lineage: $17.5M NXP-led Series A, TSMC N2 test chip, GF FDX access. ReRAM 'loses to Toggle MRAM everywhere except magnet immunity' (§0), and the Weebit licence is non-exclusive, with fabless-startup fees unknown against a $0.1–2.6M precedent. The seed gate (run-rate ≥ $250k plus gain-cell silicon ≥ 1.2×) can be met with services revenue and no product pull. The study's T2 constraint is satisfied by a paper option.

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* A named design partner (defense prime, SkyWater/IonQ, or a secure-boot customer) signing an LOI of at least $75k for a sky130 gain-cell or ReRAM macro. Plus an FTO read that clears a 2T/3T cell design around the RAAAM/Bar-Ilan claims.

**Response — ACCEPTED-KILL:** Accepted. On its own numbers the T2 stage adds at most 1.25-1.43x bitcell density in same-rule silicon, and ~0.75-0.95x against sky130's pushed-rule 6T (RT-PHY-DD5-03). Even after the downgrade to ~2.7x, the wedge's banking gain is larger. RAAAM holds the commercial seat (NXP-led $17.5M Series A, TSMC N2 test chip, GF FDX access). Weebit's licence is non-exclusive, with an unknown fee for a startup. The seed gate could have been met on services run-rate with no product pull. No named design partner or $75k LOI exists, and the FTO read was never done. T2 (C-32 and C-22) is killed. DD5 is now C-33 only, and this deep dive cannot fill the study's T2 slot.

*Action taken:* T2 legs killed across sections 0, 2.8, 7, 8, 9 and 11. Model section 9 is printed as 'KILLED, reference only' and is excluded from every headline. No external capital is planned.

### RT-HYP-DD5-01 — MAJOR — HYP — §2.2 market / §2.7 / INPUTS outreach_contacts_month_parttime 30, close_rate_of_replies 0.15

> BASE ends with 30 active accounts against a cap of 72, so a part-time founder wins 42% of every account that could exist, against free OpenRAM, OCD and Chips4Makers. The 30 contacts a month have no universe cap: about 1,000 contacts in total against about 300 projects a year, of which 36 pay. The funnel recycles the same names. At half the close rate, ARR at m36 is $110.9k (my run), and that case still implies about 29% share.

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* Track (i) passes, and at least 100 of the 120 kill-test contacts are distinct funded teams rather than hobby tiles.

**Response — ACCEPTED-DOWNGRADE:** Accepted. I re-ran your case on the Phase 4 inputs. Halving only the outbound close rate gives ARR m36 $110.9k, your number; halving inbound too gives $71.5k, with the founder never paid. The funnel did recycle names: 30 contacts a month is 360 a year against 298 projects. The revised model caps outbound contacts at distinct new project teams, 228/12 = 19 a month, and the 120-name list is spent in the m0 kill test. Going full-time no longer adds contacts. With all revisions, BASE share of the cap at m36 falls from 42% to 28% (15.5 of 55 accounts). Revised close x0.5 gives ARR m36 $36.6k. Kill-test (i) now counts deposits and LOIs only from distinct funded teams, and at least 100 of the 120 contacts must be such teams.

*Action taken:* Model R11 (OUTREACH_UNIVERSE_CAP False -> True, 19 contacts/mo). Section 2.3 outbound 1.12 -> 0.71 accounts/mo; LTV/CAC 12.3x -> 6.2x. Section 10 pass rule (i) restricted to distinct funded teams. Close x0.5 added to section 2.6.

### RT-HYP-DD5-02 — MAJOR — HYP — §4 pricing ('Characterized licence: measured data, PVT corners') / INPUTS price_characterized_macro_license $20k

> Open MPW shuttles (wafer.space, ChipFoundry) deliver typical-process wafers from a single lot, with no SS/FF corner splits. 'PVT corners' can therefore cover only voltage and temperature, and one lot gives no yield statistics. A commercial or SBIR buyer going to production needs foundry-qualified compiler views with corner data, which SkyWater bundles on S130 (§2.2). Removing licences cuts m36 run-rate from $424k to $337k and pushes full-time to m25 (my run).

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* A shuttle operator confirming corner-split wafers for MPW slots, or a buyer LOI at $20k that accepts TT-only, voltage-and-temperature-only characterization.

**Response — ACCEPTED-DOWNGRADE:** Accepted. My searches found no MPW offering skew-corner wafers, and OpenRAM's own sky130 configuration characterizes SS/TT/FF in simulation (MPW-3 config file), which the free tool already provides. Your licences-off run reproduces on Phase 4 inputs: run-rate m36 $337.5k and founder full-time at m25. The licence is restated as one nominal MPW lot measured at voltage and temperature points with rented thermal forcing, process corners simulated and correlated to that lot, yield on 50 or more die, and a warranty limited to re-delivery or a refund of fees. It is repriced from $20k to $12k, and attach falls from 0.25 to 0.15. Removing licences entirely now costs only $11.2k of revised m36 run-rate ($164.1k -> $152.9k), because the line is small. No LOI yet shows TT-only data is worth $12k; (ii) now tests it.

*Action taken:* Model R5 (P_LIC $20k -> $12k) and R6 (0.25 -> 0.15 licences/commercial account/yr). Licence restated in the section 0 pitch, section 1 diagram, section 2.3 and section 4. Section 10 (ii) requires buyers to accept the restated licence. 'Licences removed' added to section 2.6.

### RT-HYP-DD5-03 — MAJOR — HYP — INPUTS academic_share_of_accounts 0.60 / §3 rows 3, 6, 8

> Commercial accounts carry the economics. At m36, 12 commercial accounts at $800 make up about $9.6k of the $13.1k subscription MRR, plus every $10k service and $20k licence. The named commercial targets are microfirms (Avalon, Cloneless, Tillitis/Zedulo). Funded SBIR teams going to production move to the S130 NDA PDK with SkyWater's bundled compilers. At a 20% commercial share, ARR at m36 is $100.7k, run-rate $273k, and full-time slips to m32 (my run).

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* At least 2 commercial or SBIR LOIs of $10k or more from teams that confirm in writing they will stay on the open PDK for production.

**Response — ACCEPTED-DOWNGRADE:** Accepted. Reproduced on Phase 4 inputs: academic share 0.80 gives ARR m36 $100.7k, run-rate $273.3k and full-time at m32, matching your run. The named commercial targets are microfirms. SkyWater's S130 bundles SRAM compilers and embedded-NVM options (SkyWater/Infineon release, 2025-07-29), so a funded SBIR team going to production has a free path off the open PDK. Revised BASE uses academic share 0.70 (commercial cap 16 accounts). On revised inputs, 0.80 gives ARR m36 $55.1k, with the founder never paid. Kill-test (ii) now needs 2 (was 1) commercial/SBIR LOIs of $10k or more, each confirming in writing that it stays on the open PDK for production.

*Action taken:* Model R4 (ACAD_SHARE 0.60 -> 0.70); academic share 0.80 case added to section 2.6. Section 10 pass rule (ii) tightened. Section 2.2 note on S130 bundling.

### RT-HYP-DD5-04 — MAJOR — HYP — §10 pass rule (iv) / INPUTS free_bundle_shock (month 18, sensitivity only)

> A free release is the base case, not a tail risk. OpenRAM shipped v1.2.50 on 2026-10-27 after 116 commits in 12 months, OCD committed on 2026-10-30, and Chips4Makers taped out SRAM test chips in Jul-2026 (§5). Test (iv) passes if no channel owner 'confirms' a free release. Open-source maintainers don't commit to roadmaps on request, so silence will pass the gate. Under the model's own shock, the founder is never paid and ARR at m36 is $50.0k.

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* Written statements from UCSC VLSIDA and OCD that they will not publish silicon-characterized macros of 16 KB or more under an open licence within 18 months, or an exclusive contract with both.

**Response — ACCEPTED-DOWNGRADE:** Accepted. Silence would have passed test (iv); that was a design flaw. (iv) now requires written statements from both UCSC VLSIDA and OCD that they do not plan silicon-characterized 16 KB+ open macros within 18 months, or a signed exclusivity with whichever does not give one. No answer = FAIL. 'Free' is also now in the base case. OCD and Chips4Makers already have GF180 SRAM silicon, so every scenario carries an expected-value haircut from m24. Its weight is 0.39: the GF180 share of projects (0.65) x P(free GF180 macros) 0.6. It is applied to close rate, churn, subscription price and licences through the free-shock parameters. Without it, revised BASE ARR m36 would be $86.1k; with it, $65.7k. A full shock gives $34.0k (Phase 4: $50.0k). I estimate the strengthened kill test passes with ~15-25% probability, the main reason the EV is low.

*Action taken:* Section 10 pass rule (iv) rewritten (silence = FAIL). Model R13 (P_GF180_FREE 0 -> 0.6; partial free release in every scenario from m24). Section 11 #7 split: a sky130 release stops spend; a GF180 release is already in the base case.

### RT-PHY-DD5-01 — MAJOR — PHY — §2.3 line C 'Characterized licence: measured data, PVT corners, 12-month warranty' / INPUTS: license_warranty_reserve_share, license_contract_hours

> An MPW slot gives die from one lot at one point in the process window, with no FF/SS/FS/SF corner-split wafers. 'Measured PVT corners' is therefore one measured point plus SPICE extrapolation, and the sky130 foundry bitcell uses 'core memory' rules with fixed OPC and DRC waivers (OpenRAM sky130 paper, NSF PAR 10495552), its least-validated part. For a 32 KB macro (262,144 bits) at 99% macro yield, per-bit failure must be below ~4e-8 (~5.4σ), and a few dozen MPW die cannot verify that. The 10% warranty reserve and the 30 'PVT corner check' hours rest on no data.

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* SkyWater or ChipFoundry confirm corner-split wafers for MPW slots, and the special SRAM device models carry Monte Carlo mismatch data. Or the licence is restated as 'measured at nominal lot X; corners simulated', with yield measured on ≥50 die and the warranty limited to re-spin support.

**Response — ACCEPTED-DOWNGRADE:** Accepted. One MPW lot is one point in the process window. Your arithmetic is right: 262,144 bits at 99% macro yield needs per-bit failure below ~4e-8 (~5.4 sigma), which a few dozen die cannot verify. The licence is restated as 'measured at nominal lot X; process corners simulated and correlated to that lot; yield measured on 50 or more die; warranty limited to re-delivery/re-spin support or a refund of fees', with no macro-yield claim beyond the measured die count. Price falls from $20k to $12k. The 10% reserve now covers refunds only. Kill-test (iii) adds a 1,000-point bitcell Monte Carlo, which tests on days 1-20 whether the special SRAM device models carry usable mismatch data.

*Action taken:* Model R5/R6 (licence $12k, 0.15 attach). Licence text restated in sections 0, 1, 2.3 and 4. Section 10 step 1 and (iii) add the Monte Carlo. Section 11 #4 requires 50 or more tested die.

### RT-PHY-DD5-02 — MAJOR — PHY — §8 m9-m13 characterization / §11 kill 3 ('access time > 1.5× simulated') / INPUTS: one_time m9 ($4k bench), m10/m13 ($5k, 40 h)

> SRAM access time in the ns range cannot be measured through package pins or Caravel-class harness I/O running at tens of MHz. It needs on-chip BIST with programmable delay or ring-oscillator timing structures designed into the test chip, which the 60 h build-out does not include. Temperature characterization from -40 to 125°C needs thermal forcing, which neither the $4k 'FPGA carrier + PCB' bench nor the 40 h contractor provides. As budgeted, kill criterion 3 cannot be evaluated, and 'measured timing', the paid differentiator over free macros, cannot be produced.

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* A test-chip plan with on-chip BIST and delay-line timing measurement, plus a quote for thermal-forcing rental, both costed in the model with the cash low recomputed.

**Response — ACCEPTED-DOWNGRADE:** Accepted, and both items are now costed:
- on-chip BIST plus programmable delay-line / ring-oscillator timing structures, 20 contract hours at $150 = $3k per test chip (GF180 at m3, sky130 at m6);
- thermal-forcing rental at ~$2.5k per characterization campaign [ESTIMATE, ThermoStream-class], at m11 and m24 (sky130 and the second campaign).

With the other revisions, these drive BASE cash to -$8.2k at m11 on the Phase 4 schedule, beyond the $40k deployable. The model therefore gates every deferrable spend so that cash after paying it stays at $2.5k or more. Recomputed BASE cash low: $4.0k at m15 (Phase 4: $20.4k at m7). The cost is timing: the first sky130 slot waits from m7 to m15, and sky130 silicon arrives at m24 instead of m13. The access-time kill criterion can now be evaluated.

*Action taken:* Model R15 (one-time list 11 items/$68.5k -> 17 items/$94.6k, including BIST/delay-line structures and thermal rental) and R16 (cash gate, $2.5k floor). Section 2.4 cash low $20.4k@m7 -> $4.0k@m15. Section 8 roadmap re-timed. Section 11 #4 is now BIST-measured.

### RT-PHY-DD5-03 — MAJOR — PHY — §2.1 gain-cell density row / §11 kill 10 (≥1.2× vs 1.896 µm²)

> The 1.896 µm² sky130 6T cell is a foundry core-memory cell built on rules and DRC waivers that a startup's new 2T/3T/4T gain cell does not get. Unless SkyWater qualifies the cell (a foundry engagement, not an STTR deliverable), it must pass the standard logic deck, under which a gain cell is plausibly no smaller than the foundry 6T. The BIU 20-30% figure compares cells drawn under the same rules. Kill 10 is the right test, but it is scheduled after a $305k STTR when it is a two-day layout exercise.

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* A sky130 gain-cell layout that is clean on the standard DRC deck at ≤1.58 µm² (1.2× density), including its share of read-reference and refresh periphery, produced before the STTR proposal.

**Response — ACCEPTED-KILL:** Accepted; my Phase 4 notes already said a logic-rule gain cell 'may land near parity'. A rough sky130 logic-rule count for an NMOS-only 3T cell is ~3 contacted poly pitches x two diffusion rows, about 2.0-2.5 um2 before refresh periphery. That is ~0.75-0.95x the density of the 1.896 um2 pushed-rule 6T [ESTIMATE, not a layout]. The BIU 1.25-1.43x compares cells drawn to the same rules. Scheduling the density check after a $305k STTR was backwards. With no density case, C-32 is killed on sky130. Revival requires a standard-DRC-clean layout of 1.58 um2 or less, including its share of read-reference and refresh periphery, produced before any proposal (~16 contract h, ~$2.4k, not funded).

*Action taken:* C-32 leg killed. Section 2.1 gain-cell row annotated with the logic-rule estimate. Section 11 #10 rewritten as a revival gate with the logic-DRC layout test first.

### RT-PHY-DD5-04 — MAJOR — PHY — §1 'later options (T2)' gain cell / §11 kill 10 (density-only gate)

> Gain-cell eDRAM earns its place at ≤28 nm, where 6T leakage dominates; RAAAM targets TSMC N2 and GF FDX. At 130 nm, 6T standby leakage is small, and a gain cell pays refresh energy and availability loss. Its retention, 'microseconds to milliseconds' in the DD's own words, is set by the worst-tail cell at maximum temperature, where subthreshold and junction leakage are many times their 25°C values. The C-32 gate checks density only, so a cell with tens of µs retention at 85°C passes.

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* Post-layout Monte Carlo or silicon showing ≥1 ms worst-bit retention at 85°C, and refresh plus leakage power below measured sky130 6T standby power at ≥16 KB, added to kill 10.

**Response — ACCEPTED-KILL:** Accepted. At 130 nm, 6T standby leakage is small, so the gain cell's leakage advantage is small too. My own notes put 130/180 nm gain-cell retention at tens of microseconds to ~1 ms, shrinking at 85-125 C. That means constant refresh and lost availability. A density-only gate would have passed a cell with tens of microseconds of retention at 85 C. C-32 is killed. The revival gate also requires 1 ms or more of worst-bit retention at 85 C (post-layout Monte Carlo or silicon), and refresh plus leakage power below measured sky130 6T standby at 16 KB or more.

*Action taken:* C-32 leg killed. Section 11 #10 adds the retention and power conditions. Section 2.8 states the reason.

### RT-PHY-DD5-05 — MAJOR — PHY — §1 C-22 axis ('magnet immunity') / §5 'ReRAM against Toggle MRAM' / §11 kill 11

> The one axis on which ReRAM beats Toggle MRAM, magnet immunity, is shared by antifuse/eFuse OTP and charge-storage NVM, which are licence-free or foundry-standard. Key-storage sockets write rarely, at provisioning, so ReRAM's rewritability buys little over OTP. ReRAM also needs forming, write-verify and ECC for its resistance-distribution tails, and has retention drift at high temperature. Everspin 16 Mb parallel parts are rated to 8,000 A/m (AN 02049), so 'magnet immunity' matters only above ~10 mT, a requirement the DD attaches to no named buyer.

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* One named defense secure-boot program stating a magnetic-field requirement above 8,000 A/m and a rewrite need that rules out OTP or eFuse.

**Response — ACCEPTED-KILL:** Accepted. SkyWater's 2025-07-29 Infineon IP release says S130 already offers embedded NVM options alongside its SRAM compilers (the snippet does not name the technology). Antifuse/eFuse OTP also covers provisioning-time keys; the Tillitis/Zedulo GF180 chip already uses eFUSE. Everspin's AN02049 gives 8,000 A/m write immunity for 16 Mb parallel parts and 12,000 A/m for serial. ReRAM adds forming, write-verify, ECC and high-temperature drift. No named buyer states a field requirement above 8,000 A/m plus a rewrite need. C-22 is killed.

*Action taken:* C-22 leg killed. Section 1 C-22 axis struck; sections 2.8, 3 #6 and 6 annotated. Section 11 #11 rewritten as a revival gate: a named program above 8,000 A/m, a rewrite need that rules out OTP/eFuse/S130 eNVM, an LOI of $75k or more, and 3-lot plus JESD47 quotes.

### RT-VC-DD5-03 — MAJOR — VC — §2.5 revenue mix / §9 'single point of failure' / INPUTS contract_vlsi_rate_per_hour=125

> This is a staffing agency for OpenRAM, not an IP company. Every service and licence sale consumes $125/h contract VLSI hours (44% GM academic services, 58% commercial), and recurring revenue is only 30–37% of the mix. The founder is not a chip engineer and cannot judge a timing sign-off they are warrantying. The single point of failure is the contract memory engineer, and the best candidate, Open Circuit Design, is also the likeliest free competitor. The supplier can cut out the founder whenever it likes.

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* A signed exclusive with the characterization partner, or at least a non-compete covering free large sky130 macros. Or a technical cofounder with equity before m6.

**Response — ACCEPTED-DOWNGRADE:** Accepted. Revised revenue is 29-40% recurring. Service gross margin falls to 34% (academic) and 51% (commercial) at the revised $150/h. The founder cannot judge the sign-off they sell. Two mitigations, both raising cost:
(a) a new m3 kill criterion requiring a signed 12-month exclusivity / non-compete from the memory contractor on free silicon-characterized 16 KB+ sky130 macros, or a technical cofounder with equity;
(b) a contract rate of $150/h to pay for that exclusivity [ESTIMATE: +20%].

OCD may refuse a non-compete, because its business is open source; the fallback candidates are OpenRAM alumni or Chips4Makers. Either way this is a services shop, not an IP company.

*Action taken:* Model R8 (CONTRACT_RATE $125 -> $150). Section 11 #2: new m3 kill criterion (exclusive SOW or technical cofounder by 2027-02-28). Section 9 team table updated. Section 2.5 now says 'staffing agency for OpenRAM is close to the truth'.

### RT-VC-DD5-04 — MAJOR — VC — §2.7 #7 shuttles / INPUTS one_time_spends (slots at m4, m7, m16, m19)

> The funnel and the 'one shuttle cycle' moat both ride a fragile shuttle supply. Efabless is gone (01_LANDSCAPE §0 item 12), ChipFoundry runs only when a shuttle is at least 50% full, wafer.space Run 3 is only 'coming soon', and SkyWater has been IonQ-owned since 2026-07-31. The model fixes slot dates at m4, m7, m16 and m19 and characterization at m10 and m13, with no slip case. A 6-month slip on the m7 ChipFoundry slot moves sky130 silicon past m19, which erases the claimed head start and delays the only line with SBIR and DoD buyers.

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* Confirmed 2027 shuttle calendars with dates from ChipFoundry and wafer.space, plus a model variant with a 6-month slip on the m7 slot that still pays the founder by m30.

**Response — ACCEPTED-DOWNGRADE:** Accepted in substance. Searches found no 2027 ChipFoundry or wafer.space calendar. TTSKY25b took ~8 months from submission close to expected chips, and ChipFoundry slips under-filled runs. sky130 characterization moves from m13 to m16 before the cash gate. With the gate, the first sky130 slot waits to m15 and silicon to m24. Your requested variant:
- on Phase 4 inputs, a 6-month slip of the m7 slot still pays the founder at m21, inside your m30 test;
- on revised inputs the slip changes nothing (ARR m36 $65.7k), because the gate already holds the slot to m15, and the founder is never paid for other reasons.

The 'one shuttle cycle' head start is withdrawn.

*Action taken:* Model R9 (GF180 characterized m10 -> m11), R10 (sky130 m13 -> m16), and a sky130_slip variant in sensitivity (section 2.6). Section 5 win condition #1 rewritten. Section 10 asks ChipFoundry and wafer.space for dated 2027 calendars. Section 11 #6 moved to m25.

### RT-VC-DD5-05 — MAJOR — VC — §2.2 'Commercial NDA customers are out of reach' / §3 customer #2 / INPUTS academic_share_of_accounts=0.60, churn_academic_month=0.03

> The paying commercial tier ($800/month, $10k services, $20k licences) is structurally thin. SkyWater S130 bundles NDA SRAM compilers, foundry-sponsored compilers cost designers $0, and Mobile Semiconductor serves DoD on RH90. That leaves 60% academic accounts at $200/month and $3k services, churning 3% a month as students graduate. Customer #2, the Synopsys–GF GF180MCU pilot with 40 universities, is a vendor-run program. If it ships Synopsys or GF memory IP to members, the largest academic block is already served free.

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* At least one commercial or SBIR buyer committing $10k or more (criterion ii) who is on open sky130 and cannot use SkyWater's NDA compiler. Plus written confirmation of what memory IP the Synopsys–GF pilot provides.

**Response — ACCEPTED-DOWNGRADE:** Accepted. Academic share rises to 0.70 (RT-HYP-DD5-03); revised BASE has ~11 academic and ~5 commercial accounts at m36. I could not confirm what memory IP the Synopsys-GF pilot provides, so the kill test now asks Synopsys / GF University Partnership in writing. If the pilot gives members GF180 memory IP of 8 KB or more free, its 40 projects come out of the market (228 -> 188 a year) before any GF180 slot spend. Kill-test (ii) now requires 2 or more open-PDK commercial/SBIR buyers committing $10k or more who confirm in writing they will not move to SkyWater's NDA compiler for production.

*Action taken:* Model R4 (ACAD_SHARE 0.60 -> 0.70). Section 10 step 4 adds the Synopsys-GF memory-IP question. Section 11 #9: new market-removal rule. Section 10 (ii) tightened to 2 buyers with written open-PDK confirmation.

### RT-VET-DD5-01 — MAJOR — VET — §2.7 #5 / §10 PASS (iii) ('timing-clean ≥16 KB in ≤200 h', confirmed by a 16-h desk review)

> OpenRAM's own funded university team has had sky130 since MPW-1 (2020-21) and has silicon-verified only 1 KB; the 16 KB attempt 'left only a log' [VERIFIED-git per DD]. Large arrays at 130 nm fail on things a desk review of simulated Liberty files will not show: bitline leakage across 256-512 rows, and sense-amp offset vs. bitline swing under mismatch, which needs bitcell Monte Carlo to ~5σ for 131k-262k bits. Replica-timing tracking across corners and array IR drop fail the same way. Criterion (iii) passes on an opinion. The real test comes at m10/m13 silicon, after services and pre-silicon macros have been sold.

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* Before 2026-12-01: a post-layout extracted SPICE read/write of a 16 KB macro passing at SS/0.9xVdd/125°C and FF/1.1xVdd/−40°C, with a bitcell Monte Carlo of 1,000+ points, signed by the contractor.

**Response — ACCEPTED-MITIGATED:** Accepted. A 16 h desk review of simulated Liberty files was an opinion gate. My searches found only a 1 KB silicon-verified OpenRAM sky130 macro (NSF PAR 10495552) and 4/8 KB OpenRAM test-chip configurations taped out on MPW-3 in 2021, with no published measurements of the larger ones. So the gap is real, and so is the buildability risk. Test (iii) now requires, before 2026-12-01, a contractor-signed post-layout extracted SPICE read/write of a 16 KB sky130 macro at SS/1.62 V/125 C and FF/1.98 V/-40 C, with a bitcell Monte Carlo of 1,000 points or more. If the sky130 SRAM special-device models lack usable mismatch data, (iii) fails. Contractor time rises from 16 h to 40 h ($6.0k at the revised $150/h), and the kill test from ~$5.1k to ~$9.1k, still under the $10k cap and now carried in the model at m1. Pre-silicon macros are sold labelled 'simulated, not silicon-proven'. This does not remove the silicon risk at m11/m24, but the business no longer sells on an opinion.

*Action taken:* Rewrote section 10 step 1 and pass rule (iii) to require extracted-SPICE corners plus a 1,000-point Monte Carlo. Kill-test budget $5.1k -> $9.1k, added to the model at m1 (R15: +$8.1k kill-test item). Section 4 preview library labelled 'simulated, not silicon-proven'.

### RT-VET-DD5-02 — MAJOR — VET — §2.3 line C 'Characterized licence: measured data, PVT corners, 12-month warranty' / INPUTS characterization 40 h ($5k) + $4k FPGA bench

> PVT corners cannot be characterized from an MPW. Shuttle wafers are typical-corner lots with no FF/SS/FS/SF skew wafers, so corners must be inferred from simulation, which is exactly what free OpenRAM provides. A $4k FPGA carrier has no thermal forcing, and −40/125°C needs a ThermoStream or chamber time that the model does not carry. '≥80% of tested die' on a few dozen die from one lot says nothing about yield or Vmin tails on the customer's later lot. A warranty on that data is either worthless (capped at the fee) or uninsurable, so the licence's edge over free is mostly marketing.

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* A characterization plan that either buys SkyWater/GF skew-corner wafers or states plainly 'TT measured, corners simulated' and re-prices the licence, with thermal test equipment added to the model.

**Response — ACCEPTED-DOWNGRADE:** Accepted; this is the same finding as RT-PHY-DD5-01 and RT-HYP-DD5-02. An MPW gives one typical lot with no FF/SS/FS/SF skew wafers, and buying skew-corner wafers is not a T0 option. The plan now says plainly 'TT measured, corners simulated': one nominal lot measured at voltage and temperature points, process corners simulated and correlated to that lot, yield on 50 or more die, and a warranty limited to re-delivery or a refund of fees. The licence is repriced from $20k to $12k, and attach falls from 0.25 to 0.15 licences per commercial account a year. Thermal forcing (ThermoStream-class rental, ~$2.5k per campaign [ESTIMATE]) and BIST/delay-line test structures are now in the model. The licence's edge over free is honestly smaller, so the line falls to about $0.9k a month at m36 in revised BASE.

*Action taken:* Model R5 (P_LIC $20,000 -> $12,000), R6 (LIC_RATE_COM_YR 0.25 -> 0.15) and R15 (thermal forcing $2.5k at each characterization; BIST structures). Licence restated in sections 0, 1, 2.3 and 4. Section 11 #4 now tests 50 or more die with BIST-measured timing.

### RT-VET-DD5-03 — MAJOR — VET — §2.1 gain-cell density 1.25-1.43x / kill criterion #10 (≥1.2x vs the 1.896 µm² 6T)

> The 1.896 µm² sky130 6T cell is a foundry SRAM cell drawn to pushed (waivered) SRAM rules. Published gain-cell density gains are measured against logic-rule 6T in the same node. A novel 2T/3T cell on an open MPW will be drawn to standard logic DRC, because SkyWater will not grant pushed-rule waivers for it. It is unlikely to beat 1.896 µm² at all, even before refresh logic is added. Sky130 gain-cell retention at 85-125°C will be in microseconds to milliseconds, so refresh is constant and 'drop-in SRAM' is false. C-32 will probably fail its own criterion; check that before writing an STTR around it.

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* A DRC-clean (logic rules) sky130 gain-cell layout of 1.58 µm² or less (1.2x) with simulated retention of 100 µs or more at 125°C, produced before the STTR proposal (m6-m9).

**Response — ACCEPTED-KILL:** Accepted; same finding as RT-PHY-DD5-03/04. A novel 2T/3T cell on an open MPW gets the standard logic DRC deck. My rough sky130 logic-rule estimate for an NMOS-only 3T cell is 2.0-2.5 um2 before refresh logic [ESTIMATE, not a layout], against the 1.896 um2 pushed-rule 6T. My own Phase 4 notes said 'near parity unless the foundry grants rule waivers'. Retention at 85-125 C in the microsecond-to-millisecond range makes 'drop-in SRAM' false. C-32 is killed on this PDK. If it is ever revived, the first deliverable is your test: a logic-rule DRC-clean layout of 1.58 um2 or less with simulated retention of 100 us or more at 125 C. PHY's 1 ms at 85 C and power test come next, all before any STTR proposal.

*Action taken:* C-32 leg killed (sections 0, 2.8, 7, 8, 9). Section 11 #10 rewritten as a revival gate starting with the logic-DRC layout and retention tests. Model section 9 printed as KILLED, reference only.

### RT-VET-DD5-04 — MAJOR — VET — §1 / §2.2 '298 projects/yr on U.S.-fab open/academic shuttles' / Rule-4 axis 'U.S.-fabbed ASIC'

> wafer.space (108 of the 298 projects/yr) is Singapore-based, and GF's 180 nm capacity is concentrated in Singapore. The 40-university GF180MCU pilot also runs on GF180MCU. DD5 cites no source placing GF180MCU open-PDK wafers in a U.S. fab, and 01_LANDSCAPE §6 lists GF's U.S. fabs as Malta (12/14 nm, 45RFSOI) and Essex Junction (90-350 nm RF/SiGe) with no GF180MCU. I could not confirm the fab either way [UNVERIFIED]. If GF180 runs offshore, about half the funnel is not 'U.S.-fab', and the supply-security axis covers only the ~90 sky130 projects/yr. That shrinks the 'U.S. Open Memory IP' framing and the DoD/SBIR pitch to that slice.

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* GF or wafer.space documentation naming a U.S. fab for GF180MCU open-PDK runs.

**Response — ACCEPTED-DOWNGRADE:** Accepted, and now confirmed rather than unverified. The wafer.space GF180MCU Run 3 page on Crowd Supply says chips are fabricated at GlobalFoundries in Singapore (search snippet, accessed 2026-11-01). The 40-university Synopsys-GF pilot also runs GF180MCU. Of the revised 228 projects a year, 148 are GF180MCU and only ~80 are U.S.-fab (ChipFoundry and Cadence sky130, plus MOSIS and the Commons). The supply-security axis and the DoD/SBIR pitch therefore apply only to that slice. GF180 buyers stay in the market on access alone, since no free macro there exceeds 1 KB. 'U.S.' in the name now describes the sky130 line only.

*Action taken:* Section 1 Rule-4 axis split into sky130 (supply security + access) and GF180 (access only). Model section 2 now prints the GF180 (148) / U.S.-fab (80) split. R7 (LIC_DOD_MONTH 0.08 -> 0.04). Source logged in p5r-open-memory-ip.jsonl.

### RT-VET-DD5-05 — MAJOR — VET — §2.8 C-22 ReRAM chiplet ('S130 mask set + JEDEC qualification + packaging' $950k) / 'defense secure-boot sockets'

> Qualifying a new NVM die is a program, not a line item. JESD47 needs HTOL 1,000 h on 3 lots x 77 units, then endurance-cycled and uncycled retention bakes (e.g., 150°C/1,000 h), ESD/latch-up and MSL preconditioning. That is 9-12 months and three wafer lots on a production S130+ReRAM flow. The Efabless ReRAM shuttle died with Efabless, and nothing shows ChipFoundry or Cadence carry the Weebit layers (01_LANDSCAPE §0 1b #3). Defense sockets also want QML-class screening and a trusted package/test flow, which open-MPW die are not (DD §7). $950k buys a mask set and perhaps one lot.

*What would change the reviewer's mind:* A SkyWater quote for three S130+ReRAM engineering lots plus a qual-house JESD47 quote totalling $950k or less, and evidence that some 2027 MPW offers the Weebit layers.

**Response — ACCEPTED-KILL:** Accepted on qualification, with one factual correction. The Cadence SKY130 MPW did offer a Weebit-ReRAM process option at $10-12k per block for its 2026 run (deadline 2026-01-16, die Jul-2026, per the SkyWater/Cadence page in the Phase 4 notes [REPORTED]). So a prototype path existed, but no 2027 run is documented. On qualification you are right. JESD47 needs HTOL 1,000 h on 3 lots x 77 units, plus cycled and uncycled retention bakes, ESD/latch-up and MSL, on a production S130+ReRAM flow: three engineering lots, 9-12 months, and a trusted package/test flow. $950k buys a mask set and perhaps one lot. The qualification line is revised from $400k to ~$1.0M, so the chiplet line goes from $0.95M to ~$1.55M [ESTIMATE]. Together with RT-PHY-DD5-05 and RT-VC-DD5-02, C-22 is killed.

*Action taken:* C-22 leg killed. Model R17 (QUAL_LOTS $400k -> $1.0M, reference only). Section 2.8 lists the reason. Section 11 #11 rewritten as a revival gate requiring a SkyWater 3-lot quote plus a JESD47 qual-house quote.
